In a very important historic year, in which we celebrate 100 years of federal constitution law, the paradigm becomes the reverse – initially satisfying the information needs and not hiding behind an official secret. It is also a question of identifying this information requirement of citizens clearly by means of a constitutionally guaranteed right in the Constitution, it is about creating an information register, but at the same time – and I would like to stress this – maintaining the functioning of the administration. I therefore first came to 4th place. In June, invited to a round table at the Federal Chancellery and spoke to experts and all concerned – with the countries, communities, cities, even NGOs dealing with it, courts, representatives of the press. ==References== In June, I held a discussion with all the parties represented in Parliament, in concrete terms with their constitutional representatives, and then entrusted the Constitutional Service with the preparation of a draft law. This first draft work has already gone a long way. It forms the basis for further discussions, and, in order to answer your question quite specifically, the plan is to send this draft law into a corresponding review of six to eight weeks before the summer break.

The Civilian Vacation and Processing Act is a very complex law with many industry-specific fines. It is precisely this change of law that we see the importance of a functioning social partnership. The social partners have jointly sought a solution to reduce winter unemployment in particular. The construction industry is a seasonal industry, and that is why these considerations are to be welcomed. The details are presented in three main points: first, the increase in the amount of supplementary benefits in the field of the winter holiday scheme from 17 percent to 30.1 percent from 1.12.2020; second, the reduction of the supplement factor in the area of bridge money from 1.5 to 0.4 in the months from December to March from 1.1.2021; third, the facilitation of the sixth holiday week after 1 040 instead of 1 150 maintenance weeks, which are 20 years instead of 22 years. In some cases it is misrepresented in the media at the age of 25, but this reduction is now from 22 years to 20 years. Overall, these measures are cost-neutral for employers and, as mentioned above, should increase the incentive to keep workers and workers in employment over the winter, and increase the attractiveness of the industry, because there is also a shortage of skilled workers. I would like to point out that the construction industry cannot be compared to any other sector. These arrangements can and will not therefore be prejudicial to all other areas of the world of work. In the construction sector, too, the unemployment rate of God has fallen by 60 percent. At the end of March we had 56 000, currently it is well over 22,000 unemployed, and 13 280 people in the construction industry are in short work – a very good value, because up to 148 000 were in short work in May. We therefore also see in the construction sector that government action is taking place. A great deal of thanks to the social partners, especially the employees of the WKO; they have been very much called for in recent months, and they have done great work. Thanks for the tireless and great commitment! [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] One word concerning the reported open positions in the AMS: They all know that this is not the absolute number of open spaces, because many businesses are looking for employees on other platforms, i.e. they shift their search to Facebook, on the direct rate, on many other platforms. So we're talking about amounts lower than the minimum pension. This is different for all other stakeholders: That is to say, it will be given a flat rate until they have a pension of 966 euros. The fictitious disbursement is deducted with an amount of 125 euros to a maximum of 198 euros. This leads to an average pension of approximately 850 euros per month. Since 2015, this Directive has not been amended. This amendment also allows us to remove the 0.5% discount, the so-called solidarity contribution. This means a level playing field between farmers' pensions and an improvement of 167,000 farms' pensions. This causes 10.2 million euros of costs. This measure is all the more urgent as the average monthly farmer's pension represents the lowest pension for all occupations. Any other professional group would not have liked this for a long time. ParlaMint-AT_2020-07-08-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00045_d8e13377	Mr President. Mr. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! Dear ladies and gentlemen at home in front of the screens! What are the issues of agenda items 15 and 16? – It's about the so-called medical list. For the ladies and gentlemen at home in front of the screens who may not know: In Austria, someone may only arrange as a doctor if he is on this list. This list has been run by the Chamber of Physicians itself since 2003, which in turn has been passed to the National Medical Chambers. Last year, the VfGH found that this approach, as it had existed since 2003, is no longer right-wing and should be repaired. With this request we repair this condition as requested by the VfGH. This has been preceded by heated debates with all stakeholders, as is always the case with such a discussion, with such a history. There are different views. There were those who said: Well, look, we're gonna keep this whole thing the way it was, it worked! There are, of course, those who wanted a complete change. At the end of the day, there is now just this change, that we say we put the judgment one to one as much as it was given to us by the VfGH. At the end of the day comes a new regulation, which says nothing but: The Federal Minister is again solely responsible for the leadership of the medical list. He can hand it over to the Federal Medical Chamber, but that's it. So there is no sharing of these competences. Very important for us Greens: There will be a transparency package in this context. That is, the list of doctors will be made transparent in the future, the whole thing will come to a homepage. Every man, woman can also see who is admitted as a doctor in Austria, or, of course, I would also like to see it become so transparent when someone is taken out of the list and also why this is done. But this is then a question of the formation or of how we can shape the whole thing. We hope that all this will be decided unanimously today, but so to speak, it will only have a lifetime until 30.6.2021; then we will have to vote on it again. Why? – Because there are just a number of questions that need to be clarified up to then, especially in terms of what we are doing with these really important data that the medical list gives us, which will also enable us to set regionally better priorities, especially in the field of medical training, but also, of course, in the planning of places – that we are also looking at regionally, in regional management: Where might there be a lack of posts? Where do you need additional specialist doctors? - Such things. It's just a matter of discussing it with everyone. What's this about? – So far, it has been regulated in such a way that the Austrian Chamber of Physicians has carried out the leadership of the stand list, i.e., the registrations and submissions from the medical list as well as the examinations of the medical qualification itself, and has then further delegated it to the National Medical Chamber. This procedure has now opposed a medical colleague, and the Constitutional Court has decided, with its knowledge, that the leadership of the standing list of doctors by the National Medical Chamber is not right. You can legally consider this wrong, but in a rule of law this is to be accepted. The competence for implementing regulations now lies in the Ministry of Health, following this knowledge, instead of the Chamber of Physicians. The Ministry could now again designate the Medical Chamber, but has decided to implement the regulation itself. In the Novelle it is now planned to extend the previous responsibilities of the Austrian Medical Chamber until 30.6.2021. At the same time, all matters of the doctor's list are to be relocated from the doctor's own field of activity to the transferred area of activity, which provokes the doctor's chamber to anger. The background to this consideration is the Federal Government's desire for data from the medical list on better planning safety, and I think that is legitimate. It concerns two Article 15 agreements which are intended to transfer to the Regional Health Fund the competence to plan, manage and finance the entire health sector. Why this time-limit? – This is a compromise between the various federal states. The change of law, i.e. the temporary administrative transfer of the duties to the doctor's chamber, will only enter into force if all the federal states and the Federal Council expressly agree to until 31.8.2020. If that's not the case, then they're 1. In September 2020, the District Head Teams were responsible for the registration in the medical list and the submissions from the medical list. Now why is the doctor's chamber concerned about this novelle? – Firstly, it does not approve of the transfer of resources from one Member State to another, and secondly, there is a concern that the countries not only want the list of positions but also the responsibilities for the training bodies and for the qualification of training, and I personally would also like to warn against this. I was until recently head of the Training Commission in the Upper Austrian Medical Chamber, and we have determined precisely how many doctors can be trained in a particular department due to figures, facts, personnel and quality criteria. For example, it can't be that a small department with three specialists suddenly train 15 doctors. After the new Medical Training Regulation 2015, this is evaluated and strictly controlled every seven years. The examination of medical qualifications and training also requires a great deal of expertise, which is present in the doctor's chamber and has been established for many years. I myself have examined frequently verified certificates from medical colleagues who have completed training abroad and now want to work in Austria as a doctor or a doctor – so be careful of these in the final effect also for patients important reviews of medical qualifications and training! In principle, however, I believe that the country's desire for more data on planning security is legitimate. I am also convinced that we will benefit well over the next few months and that we will also be able to work together to find good, reasonable solutions with which the countries, the ministry and also the standing representation can live well. However, it is always primarily about solutions that patients can rely on being treated only by top-educated and tested doctors. That must be our top goal and we can do it. Thank you.

It was the first complete collection of the entire land throughout the state territory – at least in the then – and it recorded on every property the ownership conditions, the burdens on the rights of others to these land, those who are allowed to cross the land, those who are allowed to pass water on it, those who are allowed to draw water, those who have a pledge on it. All of this was recorded in a public book and was publicly available there. This does not have to be justified and there must be no legal interest in this information either. It is a public register from which any information accessible to the public may be obtained. In another area too, the basic book law and the basic book were groundbreaking, in the field of digitalization. The basic book was already digitized in the 1980s, at that time first in the deposits, then later in the inputs, and now gradually in the field of the collection of documents. These two areas are also essential support and essential instruments to go into the future. Digitalisation and freedom of information are two essential points which we want and are going to be transferred from the fundamental book right to all other legal areas, and precisely for this reason, as before, we will now take the basic book as a bright example and bring forward these two areas.

What is it about briefly? – Already in the years 2008 and 2012 basic book novels were decided. At that time, the conditions for the use of modern information technologies in the land database were created and the name and trust order were introduced. The main objective of this novel is to strengthen the order of trustees. It is intended to be more practicable, so that the benefits of this ranking are outweighed and the paper ranking is reduced. To this end, the present draft provides for a scheme for the case of death or loss or retirement of the professional right of the trustee. Furthermore, in future, the certification of the signature on an order of order or a declaration of order by a notary of his appointment as trustees shall not be precluded. However, changes in the application area as well as in the delivery are also envisaged. In this direction, my concerns are about the novelle. While we have in the presentation the indication that it should bring relief, what is being addressed as relief in connection with delivery actually poses a danger, a lack of legal protection for citizens. My concern is simply to highlight and safeguard consumer protection rights in the best possible way. To make it perhaps clearer: Currently, the applicant will be notified of the completion of the basic book applications even if it is represented, and this draft will be abolished. Basebook decisions would no longer have to be delivered to the respective parties after the entry into force, but only to the representatives of the parties. This is argued with the reduced administrative burden as well as with savings which, in my view, are in no relation to the possible impact on consumers. With confidence, this is about 150 000 deliveries a year and the cost estimate is EUR 300 000. We know that in this area of justice, most revenue is made that taxpayers finance this not only through their taxpayers' taxpayers' money, but also through the additional fees – and they are very high – so I simply do not think it justified to take this opportunity out of their hands. It therefore seems to me that the new regulation does not appear to me to be appropriate and extremely problematic with regard to the legal protection needs of the applicants concerned. Even if the removal of personal delivery and thus the reduction of transparency and legal certainty only lead to a slight increase in the number of painting in the transport of property, the effects in individual cases might be threat of existence. I think that this criticism, which was also presented in the review procedure, should be taken very seriously. The aim of the current novelle is to strengthen this Trustee's order introduced in 2012 and to push back the old traditional paper order. However, the benefits of the trustee's orders are not only available in court, but there are also many advantages for the legal user himself. Every member of a legal consultative profession, who had to put his entire firm on the head, had to search all the files in order to search for the sacred paper order, knows what I am talking about, because losing such a ranking can have serious consequences for the clients; for example, in our firm, the originals of these ranking decisions are safely kept in a heavy steel cabinet. However, there is such a sacred paper decision in the Trustee's order that you might lose or that could perhaps disappear somewhere in the Nirwana of a postal distribution center. So far, however, there have been in practice some problems that could occur, namely, if a trustee dies or his professional qualifications rests or if he has just retired. These problems will be solved by this amendment. But one little thing has been overlooked: The government template introduces a very sensible provision, which makes it clear that the notary can also certify itself a ranking issued to him as trustees. Accordingly, however, the certifying notary should also be allowed to certify a declaration of consent by which an earlier trustee transfers the ranking to a new trustee. Otherwise, in such a constellation, even three notaries were needed, once the old trustee, then the new trustee to whom this is transferred, and then a third notary, who thus confirms the signature, and that would actually be contrary to the objective of this bill. Every expert, every expert, will tell you that we can build so many more windmills and so many hydropower plants, so many more open spaces with photovoltaic systems: We will never be climate-neutral if we do not reduce consumption. That means: Energy efficiency in mobility, industry and, above all, in the building sector, which accounts for about 16 percent of energy emissions. Therefore, it is critical that the building stock is thermally renovated, but it does not look good. According to a study of April 2020, the rehabilitation rate is at the very low target of 3 per cent currently at 1.4 per cent. The government programme is also full of will, which is really very welcome – the climate billions are also supposed to increase the recovery rate, that is what you have explicitly said, Mrs Minister – only I say to you that I do not think it is enough, because, according to the study, we need state and private investments of between 4 and 6 billion euros a year in order to renovate so much that we are 2040 climate-neutral, and for that we certainly need a bundle of measures. Above all, we need tax incentives for thermal rehabilitation. There may be several proposals: This may be the reintroduction of the tax-free rehabilitation deposit, the reduction of the time-limit for rehabilitation expenses, the increase in the existing 25 percent limit for maintenance measures or a reduction in VAT on rehabilitation measures. – We have this in the repair process, why not also during the renovation of the building? Then there is a topic in the countries: The construction regulations must be unraveled on the one hand, on the other hand, modernized, which helps us with renovations and new buildings. And we need a refurbishment offensive in public buildings. These are partly in a state that is not exemplary, and we cannot demand the private economy to renovate if the buildings of the public hand are such a desolat. Nor do I have the impression that the Minister of Finance is calling Öbag or the BIG every day and making pressure for more energy efficiency; he didn't even need a laptop for that. My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! The largest lever is not the production of energy, but the consumption of less energy. This is only possible with the countries and communities, which I am aware of, but from there we also know the Greenwashing and the declarations of intent. Until now, we have largely seen only declarations of intent by the Federal Government, but something very concrete must happen, something which on the one hand strengthens climate protection, but on the other hand also strengthens the economy, such as a variety of incentives in the area of thermal renovation, in the building sector. It is not yet about the major reform of the Energy Efficiency Act, but only about a small innovation that is necessary. Very concrete In § 15 we clarify the obligations of the Federal Government in the case of acquisition and rental of immovable property, in order to comply with and guarantee precisely Union law. This new law is already in preparation, and we have also given ourselves a correspondingly committed timetable so that everything can come into force properly. We are working consistently and cross-party on a common goal, a law that can be implemented for all, and that does not, above all, block energy efficiency, because only if we use energy more efficiently will we be able to generate even more of our energy from renewable energy sources in the future. The funding volume of the photovoltaic plants has been almost doubled, and with the first agreed investment premium law, we support environmental investments up to 14 percent. With the future renewable expansion law, we will further strengthen the dynamic expansion. All these measures stimulate the economy and contribute significantly to climate protection. We will determine our innovative path here with Minister Geessler and continue consistently, because an intelligent energy policy creates new jobs, invigorates the region and protects our environment. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2020-07-08-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00045_d8e23110	Mr Lukas Hammer came to speak. Please, honourable Member.
ParlaMint-AT_2020-07-08-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00045_d8e23135	Mr President. Thank you, Mrs. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! It has already been mentioned: Today, we are adopting a small amendment to the existing energy efficiency law in order to avoid a conviction based on an EU infringement procedure. It is much more essential, however, that we adopt a major innovation in the Energy Efficiency Act this year. The new law is intended to help us deal with much more intelligent and economical energy than we have done before. Why is that important? – We want, and I believe, that we all agree on our whole economy. To change renewable energy so that we are no longer dependent on energy imports, oil, gas and coal, which means that every year we simply throw 8 billion euros out of the window – to Saudi Arabia, Kazakhstan or Russia. We want to switch to renewable energy. What do we have to do? – We have to switch in space heat, we have to exchange fossil heating systems through renewable heating systems, and we will – we have already been addressed – put green electricity promotion on new legs with a renewable expansion law and thus invest 1 billion euros each year in the promotion of eco electricity. [[Congratulations with the Greens and representatives of the ÖVP.]] We will promote the construction of thousands and thousands of photovoltaic plants – small plants on roofs and also larger plants – and we will promote wind turbines, we will promote the operation and construction of biomass plants, geothermal and also the construction and, above all, the revitalization of hydroelectric power plants. The problem is just, and we all know this: Over the last ten years, total energy consumption in Austria has increased by 7.5 percent, with electricity consumption almost doubled since 1990. The eco electricity has also been greatly expanded since 1990, but eco electricity expansion has in principle only compensated for rising energy consumption. The windwheels we have set up over the last 20 years have in principle simply compensated for rising energy consumption, but the share of green electricity has not increased. If we do not manage to ensure that energy consumption does not continue to rise, if we do not stop this trend, we will not create the energy transition. The expansion potential is also renewable – even with hydropower we have already achieved a very high expansion potential, we cannot install infinitely many wind turbines in Austria, we all know – is limited, and therefore it is important that we deal with energy more carefully. The current Energy Efficiency Act was an attempt. At that time, we decided together – Greens, SPÖ and ÖVP. It was an attempt: We have a goal to set an ambitious energy saving target, and we have committed companies that sell energy to introducing energy savings measures. Unfortunately, the implementation of this law has been completely reduced – one must say so clearly. The implementing rules were designed in such a way that each hocus pocus was recognised as an energy efficiency measure. For example, energy supply companies were allowed to send flow limiters attached to water taps, so that less water flows, simply to households, and this was recognized as an energy efficiency measure, whether it landed in the dynamite or whether it was used. Completely absurd, for example, was that it was also recognised that diesel-deductive, i.e. something that you pour into the diesel, so that the car then supposedly has a more economical consumption – which has never been proven – was recognised as an energy efficiency measure – in specialist circles also known as grander-water for the tank. All of this has led to the fact that this law has not been able to develop the effect it was intended for and for which we have decided. That's what we want to do better in the autumn. We want to adopt an energy efficiency law that really contributes to reversing this trend that we are dealing with energy more carefully. I am looking forward to the discussion with you all, with the ÖVP, our coalition partner, but also with the SPÖ and with the other opposition parties, so that we can: Put our energy system on completely new legs, so that on the one hand we can expand the renewables we need, on the other hand, so that we can adopt an energy efficiency law and, on the other hand, a heat law that will help to ensure that the energy transition succeeds and that we deal with energy more carefully.

The city of Graz itself has undertaken to submit to human rights in all its areas and to see that the relationship in the city is a good one. [[Congratulations on ÖVP and Greens.]] ETC was founded in Graz in 1999 and since then, the human rights report has been published annually together with the Chair of Human Rights at Karl-Franzens-Universität in Graz. This human rights report clearly documents where there is still a need to catch up, where it is necessary to look at politics, the administration, and where one can continue to bring the city. Over the years, this has naturally developed, and the city has also agreed to work with the Human Rights Advisory Council, with the accession to Cities for Children, with an agenda against violence against women, which has been ratified, in the cities internationally. With this agreement we have here, it is possible to become an Unesco Human Rights Centre of category 2. This is outstanding on the one hand in Austria, but, of course, the whole effort the city of Graz has made in this area over the last 20 years. This human rights centre of category 2 of Unesco is, in addition to the human rights centre in Buenos Aires, the only one there is, and here, the previous speaker has already shown impressively, the Agenda 2030 and the New Urban Agenda are to be pursued massively. The objectives are defined at regional, cultural level, and efforts are being made at local and regional level to pursue these objectives together. For Graz, this is also an award, as this means that in the next few years the city and the country will take over 1.5 million euros to work interdisciplinaryly with many international scientists – I believe, it is also very impressive that this is really being worked together interdisciplinaryly – and that some congresses in our congress city, Graz, will take place in our knowledge city. This is another highlight of Austria, when, in addition to the Federal Capital, where many international events take place, it is also possible that in a city like Graz, more than 155 cultures are really intensively worked on these goals too. We can be very proud of this sign, ladies and gentlemen, even here in the House. This agreement – and we have already discussed it in the Committee – should also be given broad approval, not least because it is a particular concern for the city and all the political groups in the municipal council of Graz that this human rights centre can be set up in this form, as we have in the agreement today, and can therefore work internationally. [[Congratulations on ÖVP and Greens.]] Finally, I would like to say: Human rights should be an issue for all of us, and if we are discussing here, we should listen carefully, act well and be careful in the choice of words – and in all our actions and all our efforts never lose sight of human rights. Thank you.

The Council of Europe at that time adopted the Convention on the Reduction of Multi-Statehood. The goal of the Treaty was to ensure that double citizenship remains rare exceptions. Today, of course, this access is completely obsolete and no longer modern. Nor is it particularly surprising that more and more contracting partners are getting out or at least partially getting out, only the Netherlands and, of course, Austria. I would now like to illustrate an example of how absurd Austrian legislation on citizenship is in part. Two people decide to emigrate to Sweden with their child. After some time, they apply for Swedish citizenship for themselves and for their then seven-year-old son in addition to Austrian citizenship. It's not unusual: If you live abroad for a longer time, you also want to take part in political events, for example, you want to vote. So this family lives in Sweden, buys a Volvo, eats herring with Lakritz, celebrates Midsommar, throws Christbaum out of the window at Knut, what you're doing in Sweden, and one day the now 18-year-old young man finds a letter with a convocation order or a call to go to the drawing, in the mailbox. So he goes to Austria and thinks: This is a good opportunity to get to know my home, to get to know land and people better, to feel my roots! – So he goes to the test, is found to be suitable and is also called in, namely after the tent road, because one thought: Well, we have Saab and the young man also speaks Swedish – one was also very proud of it – where he is supposed to serve. He performs his military service, then goes home to Sweden and several years later he asks for an Austrian passport because his old age has expired. The Austrian authorities, on the other hand, then certify: It is not possible for him to receive a passport, because he is not an Austrian citizen, since his seventh year of life. – You have to let that break on your tongue! This case is not invented; it also employs the Austrian courts. His parents did not know at that time how many other parents also knew that the Bub, the son, when applying for Swedish citizenship, needed a maintenance notice. He didn't have it, so he was expelled from office. Now you're thinking: Well, that was an innocent mistake, you can fix it, you can also give it back Austrian citizenship!, but: No, no, no, As I said, this is still busy with the courts at the moment. The rule case is: When a parent accepts a foreign citizenship, the adult loses Austrian citizenship just like the children; and I think we can agree that this should not be the case and that this must change. For many, a second citizenship is simply important for careers or for access to social benefits. However, most of them still want to stay Austrians. Therefore, as NEOS spokesman for foreign Austrians, I have made two applications. The first request is to facilitate the maintenance of Austrian citizenship in the adoption of a second citizenship – especially for minors who cannot apply for themselves. The second request deals with the approximately 180 000 citizens living outside the EU. They also lose EU citizenship with Austrian citizenship, and according to EU law, it requires a proportionality check, but this does not yet provide for our applicable law. I hope that this Both applications are also accepted without party political controversy. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2020-07-08-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00045_d8e25790	Mr President. My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! It is already late, but this request deals with an important issue, and it is not the first time that we are dealing with this issue in Parliament. Why are we still dealing with this question? – Because it is still an unresolved question. ==References== In the 20th century there was always a German population in the area around Pettau, today Ptuj – at the time owned by the archbishop of Salzburg. In the 1910 census, 86% of the population were German. It was also similar in the southern part of the Gottscheer land. In 1247 the area was transferred from the Patriarch of Aquileia to Carinthia. It was then in the period from 1869 to 1878 that the German-speaking population in the area with its ancient dialect, the Gottscheerisch, grew to over 26,000 people. What is necessary for a minority to be recognized as an autochthonic minority, as it is said, namely that one lives in an area over generations, his culture is more than given. In 2013, the then Federal President Heinz Fischer visited Slovenia and expressed his position that he was in favour of the recognition of this group. This has also been repeated by the current Federal President Alexander Van der Bellen on his visit; and the Foreign Minister Alexander Schallenberg, sitting here, has done this the same. Slovenia is generous among other ethnic groups. There are around 7 000 people in Slovenia, and there is a smaller population, the Italian-speaking population, with around 3 000 people. [[Abg. Kassinger: Hungarian speaking!]] As many of us today still belong to the autochthonic German people's group, the figures differ. In my opinion, our task is to do something, but the more so, the smaller this group is, because it is about securing its existence and not seeing how this group disappears completely. linguistic and cultural diversity is the strength of the European Union. Just as we in the country are called upon to do something for our peoples, I also expect Slovenia to take this step. We chose not only every five years in 2012 – in the then legislative period – but sometimes the legislative periods are shorter – and now we have always expressed our position clearly with all the parties represented here in Parliament in 2020. What do we want? . We want to see this recognition on the one hand the establishment of a People's Advisory Council by also providing a balanced representation of the small groups, such as the German, that the cultural promotion that exists in Slovenia, which has not yet been paid out on the basis of Corona, is increased accordingly and that the German People's Group also has the opportunity to be seen and heard with its own media, but also in Slovenian media. Austria and Slovenia are working very well at European level, at national level, but, as I know, at regional level, as well as at regional level. We should therefore also be able to do so and encourage our Slovenian friends, so that we may experience this step towards a recognition of this group of people. The recognition of the People's Group by the Republic of Slovenia would not only give it collective rights, but also a collective dignity; that is what it is all about. I would like to thank all the political groups for their support.

This is something we have never thought of in Austria for groups of people. This diversity is measured by how we deal with the minority – and not with the majority in a country – and therefore we support NEOS. One important point I would like to mention, Minister, is that the Slovenian Republic has frozen the funding for the German-speaking cultural associations due to the corona crisis. This was about 32 000 euros; this is not a big contribution, but it is significant for a small group of people. It would be very desirable if They could say once again that such a group of people is not denied such a contribution in a crisis. That would be an important, beautiful signal. I wish you a nice evening. Thank you.
